# Alignment review evidence (2026-09-02, main c36ae12e)

Per-item evidence for documentation/design/CORE-CONCEPTS-AND-ALIGNMENT-2026-09-02.md. Fifteen finder agents; five adversarial verifiers completed (marked [V:...] per item) before the session limit ended the run; the surface-sprawl finder (a3) and the completeness critic did not run. Item ids are cited from the alignment doc, ROADMAP.md and DECISIONS.md.



# F1 — c2-why-law-triage-modules-proactive — design law "everything carries its why", triage-not-monitor, summonable modules, proactive channel

SUMMARY: The biggest cohesion problem: the why-law exists as a data model and nowhere else. `Finding` carries why_now/why_care/why_so/why_trust (findings/store.py:38-60), four detectors plus two out-of-tree producers populate it, and 95 targeted tests pass on c36ae12e — but the four whys never reach a human. `DetectorRunner` builds the `ProactiveEvent` from `title` + `description` only (proactive/detector_runner.py:152-160), the bell dropdown renders title/body (ProactiveEventsBadge.tsx:213-221), there is no REST endpoint that lists findings, the `/findings` page renders discovery-scanner "security" items via `api.getDiscoveries('security')` (pages/Findings.tsx:52), and the Dashboard (/) is CPU/Memory/Uptime/Discoveries tiles plus five discovery rows whose only action is a link to /services (pages/Dashboard.tsx:142). Halbert therefore runs two parallel "issue" systems: discovery severities (no whys, 14 pages, Dashboard, Findings page) and FindingStore findings (four whys, SSE bell only). The proactive channel is implemented end to end in code (gate → bus → SSE → useBeingEvents) but its single visual surface, `ProactiveEventsBadge`, lives inside `ContextStage`, which `HostShell` hides in compact mode — and the default shell mode is 'both' (ShellModeContext.tsx:69) so the bell is not rendered at all in the default layout; nothing auto-opens, the tray icon is static, and events never enter the conversation. Proposals are never generated in production: `generate_for_finding` is called only by `SomaticLifecycle`, which no production constructor builds (routes/agent.py:256 passes no `somatic_lifecycle`), so the Approvals page cannot show a finding-derived proposal and the MCP `approve_proposal` tool has nothing to approve; worse, MCP `get_findings`/`get_proposals` call `FindingStore.list_findings` / `ProposalStore.list_proposals`, which do not exist (verified by import), so the only programmatic reader of the whys always returns empty. Modules: five are registered and summonable from chat (LLM-emitted `invoke_module` JSON → `module_invoke` SSE → inline in AgentChat) and WhyChip provenance expansion is real for agent responses, but "one component, two containers" holds for 0 of 14 pages — no page imports any module, `HostVitals` duplicates `VitalsModule`, and `ContextStage`'s `modules` prop is never passed. The morning report is scheduled only when being.yml enables it, is not persisted, and is delivered nowhere but the (hidden) bell. Net: backend plumbing for Slice 1 ("I found a config problem and here's why") is ~70% built; the surface that the-being.md says the slices are "felt through" is ~10% built, and the pieces that exist are not connected to each other.

CONCEPT_MODEL: The design law stands as written in the-being.md §2, with one operational clarification: the four whys are properties of a Finding, and a Finding is the single unit of attention in Halbert. Every other attention-bearing object is a projection of a Finding — the proactive interrupt (a Finding's why_now + why_care, gated by severity × category × dial), the dashboard attention row (why_care ranked), the proposal (why_so + consequence + blast-radius + rollback plan), the morning report (yesterday's Findings and outcomes), and the summoned module (the Finding's evidence rendered live). Discovery-scanner results are sensor readings ("Health Checks" in the §9.2 lexicon, Eyes in philosophy.md), not attention items; a scanner reading becomes a Finding only when a detector attaches its whys. This collapses the two parallel issue systems into one pipeline: sensors (discovery scanners, config watcher, audio tagger, vision) → detectors (attach four whys) → FindingStore → ProactiveGate → surfaces.

"Triage not monitor" then has a concrete definition: the Dashboard/Overview page is the ranked list of open Findings and pending Proposals, each row showing title, why_care (consequence), why_now (trigger), and one action; telemetry is one collapsed vitals line (HostVitals), not tiles. "One click to provenance" means why_trust is a list of typed ProvenanceRef objects (path_lines / log_cursor / metric_window / snapshot_id) rendered by the existing WhyChip, whose expansion summons the evidence or config-diff module — the same mechanism agent responses already use. "Rationale persists" is SourcePrep concepts written by dismiss()/snooze()/approve()/reject() decisions and by a real POST /api/why, and read back as why_so.

Modules are the rendering layer for Findings and their evidence, not a second dashboard: a module is one React component with a data contract, mounted standalone in a center-panel page and summoned into the conversation or context stage by a `module_invoke` event whose props identify the Finding or resource. The proactive channel delivers a Finding to the conversation as a system turn (persisted, in the one-conversation store), lights the PresencePill/top-bar indicator, and — for critical severity at Balanced or higher — opens the conversation panel; the morning report is the same mechanism on a schedule. Voice remains a projection of the same events (acoustic wake today; spoken proactive advisories when the modality engine's proactive_advisory_mode is not "silent").

VERIFIER SUMMARY VERDICT: The summary is fair and its central claim is correct and reproducible: the four whys exist as a data model (store.py:50-54), are populated by 4 detectors + 2 out-of-tree producers, pass 200 targeted tests on c36ae12e, and reach no human — the event builder drops them (detector_runner.py:152-160), the frontend has zero references to any why field, the only proactive surface (the bell in ContextStage) is absent in the default 'both' shell mode and in 'browsing', proposals are never generated (SomaticLifecycle is unconstructed), and MCP get_findings/get_proposals raise AttributeError (executed and reproduced). The two-parallel-issue-systems diagnosis (discovery severities vs FindingStore findings) is accurate and is the single most important cohesion finding; the '~70% backend / ~10% surface' figures are impressionistic but directionally right. I would change four things: (1) the summary understates how dormant the proactive stack is — reflexes are never constructed in production, the VisualWatcher is accidentally gated behind morning_report.enabled, and MCP get_proactive_events also always returns [] (fresh bus), so 'implemented end to end in code' should read 'implemented, but three of its producers/readers are unreachable from any production constructor'; (2) two evidence citations are off (the Approvals page reads /api/settings/approvals/pending, not /api/approvals; there is one finding-joining endpoint, /api/approvals/proposals, but it is unconsumed) — conclusions unchanged; (3) the concept model's voice line assumes a configurable proactive_advisory_mode that is hardcoded 'silent' today; (4) the page inventory counts Memory/Jobs which are unrouted. The concept model itself is sound and founder-consistent: 'Finding is the single unit of attention; discovery scanners are Health Checks/Eyes; every attention-bearing object is a projection of a Finding' matches philosophy.md (Eyes = deterministic sensors that trigger the thinker on thresholds), the-being.md §2 per-element table, and the §9.2 'Health Checks' lexicon; it requires no backend renames (all additions: fields on ProactiveEvent, a GET /api/findings reader, wrappers on the stores), keeps commands staged-not-executed, and it is the right frame for 'finish current features first' because it turns ~15 scattered gaps into one pipeline with one reader. The one place it should be tightened: the SourcePrep-backed 'rationale persists' line must be capability-gated (home variant has no SourcePrep) and framed as the-being.md §2 demands — a thin route over concepts, never a bespoke store.

# F2 — w5-daily-driver-conversation-terminals-shell

SUMMARY: The 3-panel shell the founder ruled on (§9.6/9.7) is genuinely on main and green: ShellModeContext defaults to 'both', Cmd+B/D/J work, nav auto-shows the center, Settings renders in the center with the conversation kept open, PresencePill switches bodies by endpoint+reload exactly as §9.5 allows, the voice HUD summon and /voice full-bleed routes are wired, and all 6 shell vitest files (34 tests) plus 7 backend thread/terminal files (178 tests) pass. The continuous conversation is the strongest part of the journey and is wired in production, not just documented: routes/agent.py hands the ThreadManager into agent.process, begin_turn resolves the hidden thread, recalls a closed thread on a strong match and injects the hint, and the frontend loads the timeline on mount with day dividers, a current-topic label and a retractable thread chip. The biggest cohesion problem is that the side-by-side default fulfils the-being §3 as a layout but not as a semantic: the conversation panel knows nothing about the page beside it (PageContext.buildPageContext has zero consumers, HostRequest.context/configPath are dropped by AgentChat's drain, and SendMessageRequest has no page/context field), the agent cannot summon or navigate anything into the center (module_invoke renders 4 small modules inline; the "17 pages become modules" refactor never happened), and the proactive channel has no surface in the default state (ProactiveEventsBadge lives only in the context stage, which compact mode hides, and no proactive or terminal message origin exists in the store). So in the daily-driver default a user sees a dashboard page occupying ~60% of the window next to a chat that cannot see it: two surfaces sharing a window, not "conversation is the core, dashboard under the hood". Terminals are the second problem: the rail's /terminal page is a simulated shell ("For MVP, we'll simulate", writes "Connected to local shell", runs one-shot /api/terminal/exec), while the real PTY dock, host vitals and the proactive badge are all in the context stage that compact mode hides, so the default state has no true shell entry point; the watched-shell write path, agent pool enable, TasksColumn and YourShellRegion remain built but unwired, exactly as the overnight results said. Smaller drifts: Cmd+backslash rail collapse is promised in §9.6 but NavRail has no collapsed prop and Layout says "not togglable in this phase"; on a 13-inch display the viewport-keyed Tailwind grids render 4-column layouts into a ~560px center and Settings gets ~320px of content beside two 240px rails and a 40% conversation; and a code-block "Run in Terminal" from Host Focus pops the dashboard open because the bridge always calls setMode('both'). The verdict is: keep the founder's default, and make the center the conversation's context region (page context into the turn, agent navigation into the center, proactive into the timeline and the top bar); wire the watched shell and the pool, put a real PTY on /terminal, and cut the dead mobile sheet.

CONCEPT_MODEL: The shell is three panels: a Rail, a center page, and the Conversation, with side-by-side as the default and Cmd+D / Cmd+J / Cmd+B as the focus controls (founder rulings §9.6-9.7, merged 941cc14b + 601c2fdb). This does not overturn the-being §3; it relocates its "context region" from a column inside the engaged surface to the center panel itself. The thesis "the conversation is the core, the dashboard under the hood" survives the side-by-side default only if three links exist: the turn knows what page and item are in the center (page context injected into the message), Halbert can summon a page or module into the center the way §3 describes ("talking about storage summons the storage module"), and the proactive channel lands in the timeline and shows in the top bar so it is visible in every panel state. Today only the first half of the layout exists; the three links are stubs, which is the drift to close.

The Conversation is one seamless timeline with hidden threads owned by the server: a session id names one turn, ThreadManager.begin_turn chooses the thread, recalls closed subjects on strong matches, and every turn's model sees the thread history, recalled receipts, the continuity hint and, when the watched shell is wired, a digest of the admin's own commands. Terminals follow the founder's 2026-08-26 direction: the admin keeps a real, unsandboxed, watched shell (YourShellRegion, OSC 133 blocks written through WatchedShellProcessor), the agent runs commands in its own pooled PTYs that it reuses when idle, tasks (not sessions) are what the right column lists, and every command from the UI is staged in the composer, never executed. The rail's /terminal page should be that watched admin shell, and the context stage in Host Focus should list tasks with the aggregate StatusLight promoted to the top bar so it exists in every panel state.

VERIFIER SUMMARY VERDICT: The summary is fair and, on the load-bearing claims, correct: I reproduced every 'wired' item against main c36ae12e (shell state machine, shortcuts, Settings-in-center, PresencePill endpoint+reload, voice routes, thread manager in agent.process, timeline on mount), reproduced the test runs (frontend 8 files/43 tests, backend 178 passed), and independently confirmed the three semantic gaps — no page context reaches the turn (I also ruled out the @-mention path: resolve_mention has zero callers and the backend never parses '@'), no navigate action exists, and the proactive badge is unmountable in the default state. The cohesion verdict is right and I would sharpen it in the finder's favour: the side-by-side default is the founder's §9.6 ruling and does not contradict the-being §3, because the-being §9 itself lists 'the 17 pages -> modules (dual-container)' under Demote — the drift is that the shell redesign shipped the container without that refactor and the three links, so it predates 941cc14b. The concept model ('the center panel is the-being's context region; the thesis survives only if the turn sees the page, the agent can summon into the center, and proactive lands in the timeline and top bar') is sound and consistent with the rulings: no backend renames, no Rust, and every recommendation is finishing already-built work. Four things I would change: (1) the terminal write side is deader than reported — session_manager's parser state is never updated, so the Plan B stage endpoint always 409s and the OSC hook must be added to the user-PTY reader loop, not just a call to process_block_close; (2) TasksColumn/YourShellRegion are not in the barrel and the 'no origin exists in the store' claim is wrong (origin is free text and terminal-origin filtering already exists; the writer is what is missing); (3) two founder-ruled or anchor items are absent from the report — the §9.3 Approvals pending-count badge and the-being §3 ambient tray, both relevant to 'It tells you first' in the daily-driver default; (4) the agent_prompts.py path and the OPUS-04 sha map (35b9fc18) need correcting before the report is handed on. With those edits the step list and the wire-vs-cut recommendations can be executed as written.

# F3 — w1-first-boot-and-companion — Journey 1 (first boot / identity discovery) for the sysadmin Tauri persona and the App Store Free companion persona

SUMMARY: The biggest cohesion problem in Journey 1 is that the machine does not have one name. Onboarding writes the chosen name to preferences.yml `ai_name`; the greeting card reads that (system.py:238-262 via /api/identity) and correctly says "I am <name>"; but the LLM's identity layer reads being.yml `name` or falls back to "Halbert" (prompts/agent_prompts.py:462), the SystemIdentityAdapter feeds the model the raw hostname (context/extra_adapters.py:63-70), and the Presence Pill reads `HALBERT_DISPLAY_NAME` env or "Host" (routes/instance.py:49-53), which Tauri never sets, so a fresh install's top bar reads "Host @ workstation" while the conversation below it says "I am Macky-Mac" and the model believes it is "Halbert" living on "Erics-Mac-Studio.local". being_config.py:218 promises the being.yml name "syncs with preferences.yml ai_name" but no sync code exists. Onboarding itself is wired but is the pre-vision form the-being §11 asked about: an undismissable modal (Onboarding.tsx) asking admin name, computer name and a user-type radio nobody consumes (user_type has zero readers); it does not ask purpose or body_name, does not infer server-vs-workstation, has no model step (Journey 1 step 3), calls itself an "AI-powered Linux assistant" on macOS, runs the deep scan synchronously inside an async handler (settings.py:1052-1057, blocks the event loop) behind a fake timer progress bar, then reloads and triggers the same deep scan again in the background (App.tsx:64-75). What is genuinely good and wired: the deterministic first greeting (HostGreeting.tsx + /api/identity, live psutil/platform, chosen name, condition-derived starters; 38 targeted tests pass), the 3-panel shell defaulting to side-by-side with Cmd+D/J/B (ShellModeContext.tsx, tested), the four-domain rail, Settings in the center panel, and the PresencePill's own labels which follow §9.2. Two functional defects sit on the first-boot path: the PresencePill body switch sets an in-memory override then reloads the page, so the override is lost and switching bodies is a no-op (apiBase.ts:22-32, PresencePill.tsx:108-113; no PresencePill test), and body_name/purpose never reach the prompt (no consumer outside instance/devices routes and morning_report.py), so the singular-entity promise "You are currently at your desk body" is unwired. For the App Store companion, nothing beyond the licence/dependency build gates exists: one tauri.conf.json for all channels (identifier ai.halbert.dashboard, entitlements null, macOSPrivateApi true, sidecar always spawned at lib.rs:523, HALBERT_CHANNEL read by nothing), no client-only mode, no host-profile store with a token, no bearer auth on the REST API, and the backend spawns `ollama serve` at startup — so a sandboxed remote client is documented (macos-strategy §2, legal §4.3) but not started. Lexicon leaks on the visible surface: "Host Canvas" button in Voice Mode (TouchBar.tsx:51), "Halbert Auditory Cortex" in Audio settings (AudioSettings.tsx:165), and "Linux assistant" copy in onboarding.

CONCEPT_MODEL: Birth is the first conversation (the-being §10): the app launches, the machine reads its own vitals and greets in the first person from real data (this already works via /api/identity + HostGreeting), and while the deep scan runs in the background it asks the three things only the user knows — what to call it, what it is for (purpose, with a server/workstation suggestion inferred from the profile), and which body this is (body_name) — and offers a model if none is ready (QuickSetup). Those answers are written once, to being.yml (name, purpose, body_name, voice, proactivity), and every surface reads that one record: the greeting card, the Presence Pill ("<name> @ <body>"), the identity layer of the system prompt, and the SystemIdentityAdapter (which should lead with the chosen name and keep the hostname as a fact). The hostname is a fact about the body, never the identity; "Halbert" is the product and the fallback, not the machine's name.

The Presence Pill is identity, not infrastructure (§9.5): in Singular Entity mode switching bodies changes the dashboard data source and must survive the reload it triggers; the conversation stays the same entity. The App Store companion is that same shell pointed at a remote body with a persisted host profile and a bearer token, with no local sidecar; until a client channel, per-channel Tauri config and REST auth exist, the companion is a documented intent, not a product, and docs should say so. Multi-session tabs (macos-strategy §4) are superseded by the Presence Pill and the-being's "relationships, not tabs".

VERIFIER SUMMARY VERDICT: The finder's summary is fair and its evidence checks out almost line for line on main c36ae12e; nothing it calls broken has landed overnight (RESULTS-OPUS-BATCH touches voice, compute, pairing, terminals and chat, not onboarding, identity or the pill), and the two 'wired' items (greeting, shell default) I re-ran green (38 pytest, 23 vitest). The concept model is consistent with the anchors: birth as first conversation is the-being §10 verbatim, the Presence Pill as identity-not-infrastructure is §9.5, and treating macos-strategy's tabs as superseded follows both §9.5 and the-being's 'relationships, not tabs'. I would change four things. (1) Footing of W1-04: the-being marks birth 'not MVP' and lists it as an open question, so the first-conversation redesign must be presented as a founder decision with a cheap interim (platform-aware copy, drop dead user_type, add purpose/body fields writing being.yml, QuickSetup pill when no model) — that interim, not the redesign, is what belongs in the 'finish current features' packet. (2) The name story is slightly mis-framed: it is four sources, not three (mDNS advert adds one), and being.yml `name` already has a UI writer (BeingTab), so the fix is a read-order helper plus dual write, which also keeps the founder's no-backend-rename rule intact. (3) On the packaged Tauri app the more visible first-boot defect in the pill is the bare fetch (shows 'Halbert @ workstation' regardless of backend), which the finder missed and which should be fixed with W1-02. (4) Soften two statuses: W1-11 is an inconsistency with the capability registry, not a contradiction of a ruling; W1-14 should credit the peer bearer auth and the loopback-only gate on identity controls and scope the gap to the operator API (chat, terminal, settings). The top-3 improvements stand: single name record with every surface reading it, a Presence Pill switch that survives its own reload, and the companion explicitly parked behind a founder decision with REST auth as its precondition.

# F4 — a4-planning-coherence — global planning soundness (MASTER-TODO, state-of-work/results, HA strategy, Rust plan, ROADMAP-08-23, the-being/explorations, macOS strategy, marketing, semantic audit, experimental, founder decisions)

SUMMARY: The biggest cohesion problem is that the 2026-09-01 direction ("Rust deferred, Linux OS far future, finish and test current features") exists only as a preamble in HANDOFF-STATE-OF-WORK and as a default row on the founder sheet, while every document that actually claims to be a roadmap still says the opposite: MASTER-TODO.md:183 ("R0 can begin now"), HA-STRATEGY §9 "Now" items 1-3 (Rust crates, halbertd, MQTT bus), documentation/experimental/README.md:13-16 ("build now"), and the Rust plan's own milestone ladder (§16.6 M0 "now — 2026 Q3", M1 "2026 Q4"). There is no single living roadmap: the repo carries three "master" TODOs (root TODO.md at 0%/19 tasks pointing to macos-strategy, .handoff/MASTER-TODO.md dated 2026-08-30 whose U1-U6 rows are all done, root FOUNDER-TODO.md 2026-08-29) plus two 08-29 master indexes and a 2026-09-01 founder sheet, and documentation/README.md:22 still names root TODO.md the "Master engineering roadmap". Velocity has outrun resync: 830 commits and 176 new .handoff docs since 2026-08-23 (112 since 08-29), yet ROADMAP-2026-08-23 (phases 0-8) has never received a status line even though all eight phases exist as code on main (intake/, findings/detectors, being_config dial+voice, proactive/ + being.py events, modules/registry) — the-being.md's footer still calls it "the phased implementation plan". Against the anchors, the shell review §9 rulings (no backend renames, definitive lexicon, banned "Compute Mesh"/"Sovereign Host"/"Engaged mode") already settle the three "SEM-01..03 forks" the semantic-audit handoff and the founder sheet still present as open, and the singular-entity/§9.5 body-switch model supersedes macos-strategy §4's "multi-session tabs each saying I AM <hostname>" and the README architecture diagram. Execution state today (HEAD c36ae12e): Opus 01-05 (26 commits), SONNET-01 (merge 909b56c4) and SONNET-02 are on main; SONNET-03/04/05 have not run — I reproduced 5 of their failures in isolation (test_multi_instance, test_frontend_no_relative_urls, test_corpus_license_gate x2, test_llm_discover), BeingTab.tsx:59-108 still double-prefixes /api, AudioSettings.tsx:73/108 still fetches a GET /api/being that being.py never defines, and tier_router.py:383 still raises NotImplementedError for OpenAI while the marketing footer promises "Any LLM or BYOK". Several founder-sheet defaults were already implemented by the Opus session (Wyoming loopback+token, pairing handshake, VM-STT) or resolved contrary to the default (U3-26 was not subsumed), so the sheet is now a mix of open, implemented-per-default and superseded rows with no status column. The fix is structural, not another resync: one living ROADMAP with a dated Direction block and a per-workstream definition-of-done table, one append-only DECISIONS log (absorbing HA §8 D1-D8, shell §9.1/9.2, U6 D1-D4, standing founder directives, and the open founder rows), and per-workstream handoffs/results with a fixed status enum — with MASTER-TODO, root TODO.md, the 08-29 indexes, and the "Now" lists in HA-STRATEGY/experimental README reduced to pointers or banners.

CONCEPT_MODEL: Planning in Halbert should have exactly three living artefacts and everything else is either an anchor or a dated record. (1) ROADMAP.md — the only document allowed to say "now / next / deferred / never". Its header carries the dated Direction in force (2026-09-01: full Rust rebuild deferred; Linux OS far future; current features completed and tested first) and its body is one row per workstream: feature, definition of done (wired in production + tested, never "documented" or "code units exist"), current status with sha/results-doc evidence, owner packet, and the founder decision it is gated on, if any. "Deferred by direction" is a section, not a tier of enthusiasm: the Rust R-phases, HalbertOS appliance, Windows, and the blue-sky pillars sit there with their named revive triggers (Rust plan §16.4a) and nothing in them is scheduled. (2) DECISIONS.md — append-only, dated, one line per decision with the anchor it comes from: philosophy.md and the-being.md "decided" items (proactive dial, voice setting, one mind many hands), shell §9.1 rulings and the §9.2 lexicon (UI labels change, code names never), singular-entity mode semantics and §9.5 cross-body data story, HA-STRATEGY §8 D1-D8, U6 D1-D4/Q3/Q4, standing directives (never name a model on a user surface, never "Sovereign", the engaged surface is labelled with the onboarding name, no telemetry by default, one conversation with no conversation list), and operator-visible changes such as the Wyoming default flip. The current DISPATCH-...-FOUNDER-DECISIONS sheet becomes the "open" section of this log, each row gaining a status (open / implemented-per-default / resolved-otherwise / ratified). (3) Per-workstream handoffs and results in .handoff/, each with a status from a fixed enum (DRAFT, ACTIVE, LANDED, SUPERSEDED-BY <doc>, DEFERRED) and a pointer back to its ROADMAP row; results docs are the only inputs that move a ROADMAP row, and a single owner resyncs. The anchors (philosophy.md, the-being.md, shell review §9, the singular-entity handoff, HA-STRATEGY §3/§8, macos-strategy §2 tiering) are not edited to track status — they get dated "shipped as / superseded by" notes so the vision stays readable while the physical design (3-panel shell, PresencePill body switch) is recognised as the realisation of "the conversation is the core layer, the dashboard runs under the hood". Phase numbers are never bare: every plan that says "Phase N" is namespaced by workstream, because "Phase 7/8" currently means four different things (ROADMAP proactive/reactive slices, multi-instance switcher/light variant, singular-entity pairing UI, and the retired docs/PhaseNNN sequence still cited in app.py:295-297).

# F5 — c1-identity-voice-memory — the identity concept as built (name, voice, body, persona models, memory-as-biography)

SUMMARY: The biggest cohesion problem is that the founding ethos is not in the prompt: on main (c36ae12e) no identity layer reaches the model on any production path. The only system message a turn sends is build_planning_prompt/build_response_prompt output (agent_prompts.py:525-597, 718-736) folded into messages[0] by _build_messages (state_machine.py:1479-1530); build_system_prompt, which holds the per-voice "You are {name}… You ARE the machine" identity, has zero callers, and a probe of the shipped builder confirms neither prompt contains "You are", the configured name, the body or the voice. Everything downstream of that — the first_person/the_computer/hybrid setting, being.yml name, personality/archetypes, body_name, hostname — is therefore inert in chat, Wyoming and Voice Mode alike; the voice setting is otherwise wired end to end (UI to being.yml to reload_personality) but its only live consumers are the continuity preamble and the morning-report intro. The name itself has six sources that disagree (preferences.yml ai_name, being.yml name, HALBERT_DISPLAY_NAME/"Host", Haloysius persona_id "halbert", PersonaStore "default", hardcoded "Halbert" in modality wiring, hostname in MCP), so the chat header says the onboarding name while the Presence Pill says "Host @ workstation". The philosophy's "one identity per host, I am <hostname>" and the singular entity's "one Halbert, many bodies" are reconcilable — entity is named by the user and holds memory on the canonical host; body is the hostname plus body_name plus capabilities — but that resolution is written nowhere and body_name never reaches the prompt despite three docs saying it does. Memory-as-biography is not real on the conversation path: memory_v2 receives only promoted template thoughts (write-only, never read into a turn), the system-event → worry path is dead because DiscoveryEngine lacks the methods the mapper probes for and telemetry_store is None, PersonaCognition is never persisted, journald/hwmon ingestion feeds a ChromaDB index the agent is fenced from, and the Halbert StateStore ledger is written at thread close but never read into a turn. "How are you?" today retrieves keyword discovery hits and SourcePrep chunks for the literal words, then relies on a reactive-slice instruction plus a deterministic vitals card. What is genuinely wired and good: thread receipts/recall_thread continuity, /api/identity's deterministic first-person sentence in the UI, PersonaStore symlink switching, and the singular-entity peer memory/thread proxies. Four identity models coexist in code (BeingConfig, PersonaStore, legacy PersonaManager IT_ADMIN/FRIEND, Haloysius persona_id) and the Identity & Voice persona UI still 404s on a double /api prefix.

CONCEPT_MODEL: Halbert is one entity. Its name is the one the user chose at onboarding ("What should I call this computer?", stored as preferences.yml ai_name); that name is the single source for everything user-facing and for the prompt, and being.yml `name` either writes through to it or is removed. The hostname is a fact about a body, never the entity's name — "I am Titan; this body is erics-mac-studio.local" — which reconciles philosophy.md's "I am ubuntu-server-01", macos-strategy's "I AM <hostname>" tabs and marketing's "I am the machine": the machine speaks in first person, and what it calls itself is what its person named it. In singular-entity mode the entity (name, persona_id, memory, threads on the canonical host) is shared and each node is a body with a body_name and its own capabilities; in independent mode a node is a separate entity with its own name. The identity is carried in exactly one place at runtime: an identity block that leads messages[0] on every LLM turn (chat, Wyoming, Voice Mode, proactive/morning report) and states name, platform, body, voice (first_person default; the_computer and hybrid change only expression) and the personality section. Prompt-system v2 identity.xml/base-safety.txt and the legacy IT_ADMIN/FRIEND persona layer are retired; BeingConfig plus PersonaStore is the one persona model, and a PersonaStore persona carries its own Haloysius persona_id so switching personas switches memory.

Memory as biography is Halbert-owned (founder D1): the biography is the thread store (receipts, recall_thread) plus the machine-state ledger (continuity/state_store.py) fed by real sensors — discovery scanners, psutil telemetry, config snapshots, findings — and read deterministically into a turn before the model speaks. Haloysius memory_v2 is the mind's private stream (promoted thoughts, worries) and is not the biography; PersonaCognition state persists across restarts so a worry can outlive a process. "How are you?" is answered from an "Observed now" block assembled without a model — /api/identity facts, current ledger triples, open findings, vitals — each item carrying its source, after which the model narrates in the configured voice. That is the-being §7 slice 2 and philosophy's autobiography loop, with the retired ChromaDB step replaced by the ledger.

VERIFIER SUMMARY VERDICT: The summary is fair and its central claim is stronger than stated: on c36ae12e no identity, name, voice or body reaches messages[0] on the one production path that chat, Wyoming and Voice Mode all share (routes/agent.py:256 → state_machine.py:280/1694/2810/1517-1530), my independent probe of the shipped builder reproduces the finder's, and git shows the regression has a sha (1ecaff86 deleted the last build_system_prompt() caller and test_agent_identity.py) with the 'fix' parked on a fallback branch production never takes. Two corrections: (1) the voice setting is NOT 'otherwise wired end to end' — the hot-reload guard hasattr(agent,'prompt_builder') is false against the real state machine, so Settings changes never reach the running builder until restart; (2) the hardcoded 'Halbert' in modality_wiring.py:170 is dead code (no caller of get_modality_prompt_builder), so it is five live name sources plus one inert, not six live. Everything else (memory_v2 write-only, dead event→worry path, ledger never read, PersonaCognition unpersisted, 'how are you' = keyword hits + reactive-slice text, four persona models, BeingTab double /api, pill says 'Host') re-verified against code. The concept model is sound and compatible with the founder's rulings: it proposes a resolver and deletions, not backend renames; keeps BeingConfig/PersonaStore/federation names; puts the biography in the Halbert-owned ledger per D1; and matches the shell review §9.5 pill semantics ('Halbert @ desk' = entity + body). I would change three things: pick the one writer for the name now (BeingTab 'Name' → the existing /api/settings/computer-name route) rather than leaving 'writes through or is removed' open; settle the hybrid-voice definition before the identity block ships, since the-being §5 and agent_prompts.py:84-88 define it in opposite directions; and state explicitly that continuity/freshness.decide and SystemIdentityAdapter already exist unwired, so the 'Observed now' block and ledger read are wiring work, not new modules — consistent with the direction that current features get completed before anything new.

probe confi || M2 (doc/code contradiction): the-being.md §5 (line 130-131) defines hybrid as 'First person for state, neutral for concerns'; agent_prompts.py:84-88 LAYER_1_IDENTITY_HYBRID says first person for subjective experience/feelings and third person for obj || M3 (unwired module): continuity/freshness.decide — the MEMORY/LEDGER/OBSERVE answer-source ladder — has no production caller (only exported at continuity/__init__.py:14); # F6 — w4-multi-body-and-multi-session

SUMMARY: The biggest cohesion problem is that the repo carries two incompatible multi-machine concepts and neither is finished: Model A (one Halbert, many bodies) has real backend plumbing on main but no working user journey, while Model B (Universal Multi-Session Client with per-tab "I AM <hostname>") exists only in documentation that presents it as shipped (documentation/FEATURES.md:508-514, ARCHITECTURE.md:458-468, README.md:38/83 "Switch machine tabs", "Fleet peer mesh") with zero code behind it (no HostManager/HostConnectionContext/tab component anywhere; the only endpoint abstraction is a single module-level override in dashboard/frontend/src/lib/apiBase.ts:22-27). What is genuinely wired in production for Model A: BeingConfig fields (config/being_config.py:239-252), PeerMemoryBackend selected by cognition_wiring._create_memory_store (integrations/cognition_wiring.py:244-278) against mounted peer memory routes (routes/memory.py:200-330, app.py:279), PeerConversationStore injected by agents/threads.py:1069-1099 against the mounted /api/conversations dispatch (app.py:319), the compute endpoint (app.py:304), the Devices API and Settings > Devices page; 83 targeted tests pass (1 known-rot failure in test_multi_instance). A user cannot do Model A end-to-end today: pairing through the UI cannot complete (mDNS list hardcoded [] at routes/peers.py:590-604, manual form throws at PeerPairingModal.tsx:161-170, no component calls approvePairing, and the issued token lands in browser localStorage at DiscoveredPeerCard.tsx:80 rather than being.yml peer_token which the backend reads at cognition_wiring.py:254 and threads.py:1085); the entity-mode toggle never sets persona_id_override (routes/devices.py:262-264); body_name reaches no prompt (grep: consumers are only devices.py/instance.py); and PeerToolProxy is never injected (routes/agent.py:127). The shell §9.5 promise that the Presence Pill switches the dashboard data source is not delivered: PresencePill.handleSwitch stores the endpoint in a non-persisted variable and then calls window.location.reload() (PresencePill.tsx:108-113), which discards it, so every switch lands back on local — a bug inherited from InstanceSwitch (137f8468) and untested. The pill also shows the wrong identity ("Host @ workstation": routes/instance.py:49-53 uses HALBERT_DISPLAY_NAME, which nothing sets, or the literal "Host"/"Home") while the conversation greets with the onboarding name from /api/identity (routes/system.py:268-270, AgentChat.tsx:1094), and it reports "Independent Node" on the canonical host because singular is defined as bool(canonical_memory_url) (cognition_wiring.py:148-155). "I am <hostname>" is therefore still per-body in singular mode: the prompt carries platform.node() (prompts/context.py:263,296) and /api/identity says "I am <this body's ai_name>", so one entity introduces itself with two names depending on which body answers. Conceptually the-being.md §6 ("One Halbert per host", line 163) and the singular-entity handoff ("one Halbert across hosts") also disagree and no design doc under documentation/design/ states the reconciled concept; marketing (stops.jsx) makes no multi-machine promise at all, while README does. Planning is unsound on this axis: IMPL-PLAN-SINGULAR-ENTITY-TASKS says "Status: COMPLETE", MASTER-TODO.md has no multi-body, pairing, or remote-client line, and the HA strategy §9 near-term list does not include finishing the multi-body journey even though the direction in force is "complete and test current features first".

CONCEPT_MODEL: There is one multi-machine concept: Halbert is one mind that may have many bodies. A body is a host running the Halbert backend; the always-on body is the Canonical Host and holds the one memory (memory_v2) and the one conversation store; every other body proxies memory and threads to it (Singular Entity). A host can instead be its own Halbert (Independent Node) — a separate entity that may still lend compute. "I am the machine" generalises as: the entity's name is the persona's name (shared across bodies in singular mode, taken from the canonical host), and the hostname/body_name says where it is speaking from — "I am <name>, at my <body_name> (<hostname>)"; in Independent Node mode each host's Halbert introduces itself by its own persona name and hostname. There is one conversation surface and one active body at a time; the Presence Pill selects which body's dashboard data you are looking at and, in Independent mode, which entity you are talking to.

Model B survives only as the Remote Client: a Halbert app with no local backend (the App Store companion, or any body's app pointed elsewhere) connects to one paired body at a time through the same Presence Pill and the same pairing token. It never opens concurrent session tabs — tabs are a conversation list by another name, which the continuity direction rejected — and per-connection "I AM <hostname>" applies only when the connected host is an Independent Node. Every user-facing surface says Halbert, Body, Linked Devices, Canonical Host, Singular Entity, Independent Node; the code keeps federation/, peers_config.py, BeingConfig, and InstanceInfo unchanged.

VERIFIER SUMMARY VERDICT: The finder's summary is fair and its concept model is sound: Model A is what the founder ruled (shell review §9.2 lexicon and §9.5 cross-body data story), what the singular-entity handoff designs, and what the backend actually implements (PeerMemoryBackend, PeerConversationStore, peer memory/conversation routes, the compute endpoint, the Devices API — 130 targeted tests pass on main today), while Model B (concurrent session tabs, HostConnectionContext, hosts JSON) has zero code and survives only in macos-strategy.md, README.md and stale FEATURES/ARCHITECTURE prose. Ratifying 'one mind, many bodies; Model B survives only as a single-connection Remote Client' is consistent with the rulings (no backend renames, finish current features, Rust deferred) and with the continuity direction that rejected a conversation list. I would change four things. (1) Soften 'documented as shipped': FEATURES.md marks the section Planned by its own legend and ARCHITECTURE.md lists it under Next Steps; README.md:38/83 is the real over-promise. (2) The user-journey finding is understated: the only pairing flow that runs is inverted (the local backend pairs a phantom of the remote and the PIN label points at the wrong machine), and in the packaged Tauri app the entire Devices/pairing surface plus the pill's own info fetch are dead because of bare relative URLs (test_frontend_no_relative_urls fails today) — that bare-URL fix and require_local_admin's 'each body configures itself' constraint must be step 0 and a stated rule of the Join flow. (3) The identity story has three name sources (being.yml name feeds the prompt, preferences ai_name feeds the greeting, HALBERT_DISPLAY_NAME feeds the pill) and persona_id is an id, not a name; the concept model should say which key is the shared name and that singular mode copies it from the canonical host. (4) The model needs two missing primitives it silently assumes: a way for peers.json to mark a pairing as a body (role is only satellite/compute_provider today) so the canonical host can know it is canonical, and a decision on outbound token custody (R2-F6) before PeerToolProxy can be wired in either direction. The silent local fallback when the canonical host is unreachable deserves promotion to its own item, since it is the one failure that turns 'recall the same thread' into two diverging autobiographies without anyone seeing it.

# F7 — c4-three-roles-one-mind — philosophy's Guide/Deep Thinker/Eyes and the-being's "one mind, many hands" vs the runtime on main c36ae12e

SUMMARY: The biggest cohesion problem is that Halbert has exactly one mind and it only thinks when a human types: every background limb that was supposed to keep the being alive between conversations is dead, unwired, or fed by nothing. The Guide half is real and single-instance — one AgentStateMachine (routes/agent.py:256) shared by the dashboard and the Wyoming voice path, every turn passing through REFLECTING where the Haloysius advance_turn ticks exactly once (state_machine.py:2649-2698; 68 targeted tests pass). But the scheduler that carries the "Deep Thinker" rituals cannot register a job at all: AutonomousExecutor.schedule_cron_job/schedule_one_time raise "This Job cannot be serialized" because the SQLAlchemyJobStore pickles a local closure (reproduced in the venv), dashboard/app.py swallows it as a warning, so the 6-hourly detector sweep and the daily morning report have never run from the dashboard; two further bugs (undefined name `job`, SIGALRM from a worker thread) sit behind that one. The Eyes write to stores the mind never reads: SystemEventMapper's 30 s scan calls DiscoveryEngine methods that do not exist and is handed telemetry_store=None, ThreadManager.tick() (idle close sweep + Consolidator) has no production caller, Halbert never calls Haloysius' tracker hooks, and ingestion's journald/hwmon ChromaDB collections are read only by a TelemetryAdapter that is never constructed on the agent path — so worries/drives stay empty and the StateStore ledger has no writer on a sysadmin host. The mind also forgets on restart: PersonaCognition is built fresh and advance_turn is never given base_path, and the-being's believes/wants/worries_about/conflicted_about predicates exist in neither Haloysius nor Halbert. HomeCognitiveLoop, the only "unprompted tick" candidate, is uninstantiated, returns no decisions, and would TypeError against the production tick signature. Subagents, the somatic pipeline, ComputeRouter and freshness are unwired seams; MCP-originated actions bypass the mind entirely. philosophy.md's implementation table points at a 25-line placeholder runtime/ graph nobody imports, a removed memory/retrieval.py, and dead LLM autonomous tasks, and three architecture docs still describe a routes/chat.py that no longer exists. The recommended structural fix is one mind-owned heartbeat coroutine on the dashboard loop that replaces five competing daemon-thread cadences.

CONCEPT_MODEL: There is one mind per host and it lives in one process: the dashboard server. The Guide is the AgentStateMachine — one instance from routes/agent.get_agent(), shared by the conversation UI, the Wyoming/voice path and the persona/gpu routes; a turn runs IDLE→PLANNING→(SEARCHING|READING|EXECUTING)→OBSERVING→REFLECTING→RESPONDING, and REFLECTING runs the Haloysius cognitive tick (advance_turn) exactly once, promoting thoughts into memory_v2 (PersonaMemoryStore under HALOYSIUS_DATA_HOME) and persisting cognition (worries/drives/emotions/beliefs) so it survives restarts. Halbert-owned continuity carries memory across time: ThreadManager begin_turn/end_turn, receipts and FTS recall in the conversations SQLite, and the StateStore ledger (~/.local/share/halbert/state_ledger.db) that answers "what is true now / when did it change" — every worry the mind holds is mirrored there as a triple until resolved.

The Eyes are deterministic workers — ingestion (journald/hwmon → JSONL + ChromaDB), discovery scanners (startup scan → discoveries collection), detectors (config-watcher-triggered and scheduled sweeps → findings.db → ProactiveGate → ProactiveEventBus/SSE), state trackers, VisualWatcher and the acoustic bridge. They report into the one mind through two channels only: SystemEventMapper.add_event (becoming worries/drives/emotions at the next tick) and the StateStore ledger; the Guide reads their stores through adapters (findings, telemetry, discovery) and tools, never through a private path. The Deep Thinker is not a second personality but the scheduled, unprompted work of the same mind: one heartbeat on the dashboard event loop that, whenever no turn holds the turn lock, drains the event mapper and ticks cognition with an "unprompted" marker (template thoughts by default, LLM only under the proactivity dial/budget), runs the ThreadManager idle sweep and Consolidator, fires due sweeps and the morning report, and syncs trackers to the ledger. Hands (subagents, MCP, compute peers) are gated by the same approval/guardrail stack and write back into findings/approvals/ledger so the being knows what its hands did.

Reality on main c36ae12e: the Guide sentence above is true and tested; every sentence about the heartbeat, the Eyes feeding the mind, persistence of worries and the ledger having writers is design, not code.

# F8 — a2-config-data-memory-model — Halbert's own configuration files, env vars, data stores, XDG/data_dir handling and multi-instance isolation

SUMMARY: The biggest cohesion problem is that Halbert has no single answer to "where do my files live": two competing path resolvers (utils/paths.py = XDG/root-aware; utils/platform.py = macOS-native, ignores XDG_*) plus a dozen hard-coded ~/.halbert, ~/.local/share/halbert and ~/.config/halbert paths mean that on this Mac today config is split over two directories and data over four roots (~/.halbert, ~/.local/share/halbert, ~/Library/Application Support/Halbert/Data, ~/.local/share/haloysius), verified by running the resolvers. Worse for the singular-entity story: the hottest stores (conversations.db, state_ledger.db, somatic_blocks.db, model_outcomes.db, timeline/behavior, self_knowledge) ignore HALBERT_DATA_DIR entirely, so the documented host+home multi-instance deployment (both services User=halbert) shares one conversations.db and one state ledger while each instance writes memory_v2 under its own root — the opposite of "canonical host holds threads". The second problem is that being.yml conflates the persona (identity & voice) with body/host configuration (variant, body_name, canonical URLs, peer_token, HA credentials, security tiers, autonomy) — and because personas are separate files behind a symlink, switching persona silently swaps the node's variant, peer credential and trust tiers; meanwhile any Settings save rewrites being.yml from the dataclass and drops the untyped capabilities: section (verified in scratch), so the capability-registry override the singular-entity handoff relies on cannot survive the UI. Persona identity itself lives in three unrelated systems (PersonaStore file id 'default', the Haloysius memory key from persona_id_override/HALBERT_PERSONA_ID 'halbert', and the legacy PersonaManager enum + persona_state.json whose purge deletes a tree memory_v2 never writes), and the Identity & Voice tab still calls /api/api/persona on main. The same fact lives twice in several more places: variant (file + env + HALBERT_ROLE), entity name (preferences.yml ai_name, being.yml name, PersonaStore display_name, HALBERT_DISPLAY_NAME), HA credentials (being.yml + ha_config.json; capability probe reads only the former), peer token (being.yml plaintext + env + browser localStorage; hashes in peers.json), approvals (ApprovalEngine JSON dir + findings.db proposals), somatic blocks (own db + a table in conversations.db), and policy.yml (Tool Permissions tab writes platform.get_config_dir(), the tool gate reads paths.config_dir() — different files on macOS). Against ROADMAP §1's "three stores" decision, reality is four first-class stores (thread store + state ledger, memory_v2, SourcePrep, findings/proposals) plus a ChromaDB that is still written on every sysadmin node by ingestion and driven by the Settings › Knowledge tab yet fenced off from the agent path — a write-only store. The good news: being.yml and models.yml writers are atomic, 0600 and cross-process locked; peers.json is atomic; the agent turn reads exactly the right things (thread receipts/history, memory_v2 via the cognition tick, SourcePrep via capability, discovery) and none of the legacy stores; and the frontend's selected-body override is in-memory, matching §9.5. What is needed is one resolver, one ownership table (host vs persona vs models vs service configs), env as seed-only, and a short retire list.

CONCEPT_MODEL: Halbert has exactly one configuration directory and one data directory per instance, each resolved by a single function (HALBERT_CONFIG_DIR / HALBERT_DATA_DIR override → root → XDG on Linux → platform-native on macOS), and every file the system writes lives under one of them; there is no third resolver and no literal home-relative path anywhere in halbert_core. Configuration is partitioned by what it describes, not by which feature happened to add it. (1) The body/host file (today's being.yml minus identity; call it host.yml or a `host:` block) owns facts about this machine as a body of the entity: variant preset, body_name, capability overrides, canonical_memory_url / canonical_thread_url, the reference to the outbound peer credential, security tiers, autonomy levels and the integration connections (HA, Frigate) whose secrets are 0600. (2) The persona file (personas/<id>.yml) owns identity and voice only: name, voice, proactivity dial, quiet hours, personality, per-persona model override — switching persona never changes what the body is or whom it trusts. (3) models.yml owns model slots and endpoints (global < workspace < session, as built). (4) Service configs (audio_config.yml, vision_config.yml) stay as they are but use the same resolver and atomic 0600 writes. Environment variables are seeds and headless overrides only (deploy units, Tauri launcher), never a parallel source read in place of the file: every reader goes through one accessor per fact (file value > env > default), and /api/instance/info reports the effective value and its origin. Each fact has one home: the entity's name lives in the persona; the body's label in the host file; the HA token in the HA connection file; the outbound peer credential in one credential store (peers.json keeps only inbound hashes); the variant in the host file.

Data has four owned stores, mirroring the-being.md's three pillars plus Halbert's own continuity: (a) the thread store — conversations.db (threads, messages, receipts, open loops, terminal blocks, compact boundaries, somatic links) together with the state ledger — owned by Halbert continuity, read into every turn; (b) memory_v2 (Haloysius) — episodic memory keyed by the entity's persona_id, under the same data root, peer-proxied to the canonical host in singular mode; (c) SourcePrep — the knowledge corpus, host-config awareness, concepts (rationale) and observations; (d) findings.db — findings, proposals and approvals as the single proactive/approval ledger. Everything else is a cache or a retired artefact: ChromaDB, vision_cache, config snapshots, obs/dashboard JSON are rebuildable and never a source of truth; persona_state.json, model.json, context_preferences.json, terminal_history.json, the hybrid "Memory" collections and the PersonaManager memory tree are removed. Multi-instance isolation is a property of the two resolved roots, so two instances on one machine can never share a database by accident, and the "canonical host" is the only place a thread or memory is written in singular mode.

# F9 — w6-external-mcp-cli-developer — external MCP client, CLI, and contributor journeys

SUMMARY: The biggest cohesion problem is that the MCP server — the only channel through which "I am the machine" reaches other agents (marketing: coding agents, Claude Desktop, Cursor) — is half dead in production. A live stdio session on this macOS host (18 tools listed, 202 unit tests green) shows get_findings and get_proposals fail on every call because server.py calls FindingStore.list_findings()/ProposalStore.list_proposals(), methods that have never existed (real API: list_open/list_all/list_pending; bug present since 4499d2e8); search_knowledge fails on every call with a TypeError (passes limit=/scope= to SourcePrepClient.search(query,k,min_score,project_id)); get_discoveries and get_proactive_events are empty by construction because the MCP process builds a fresh DiscoveryEngine() and ProactiveEventBus() (in-memory, process-local); and the three config-query tools fail closed (SEC-05) with no macOS path to stage a snapshot — the only latest.json on this machine is a pytest temp file. So the Four Whys on findings (why_now/why_care/why_so/why_trust exist in the store) and the 16,000-manual knowledge never reach an external client; only vitals, being-config, autonomy and the write gates work. The gates themselves are sound: confirm + non-echoed phrase on escalation, idempotent approve, batch rejected, peer_token redacted at the choke point. Identity over MCP is the raw hostname ("halbert-Erics-Mac-Studio.local", "[Erics-Mac-Studio.local] ..." on every tool) with no first-person instructions — against the founder's ai_name rule and philosophy.md. Architecturally the MCP topology is split: the server is a standalone process (stdio / HTTP :8765, never started by any unit), while PeerToolProxy and FleetProxy post JSON-RPC to the peer's dashboard URL (:8000, which serves the SPA at "/"); ToolExecutor is never constructed with a peer proxy, so the §9.5 agent-to-tool cross-body path cannot work today. The CLI has no conversation entry: the `halbert` console script is the dashboard uvicorn server, `halbert info` errors, the GPL notice tells users to type `halbert license` (fails when installed), and Halbert/main.py's 63 subcommands expose deferred personas and legacy ChromaDB RAG but nothing that opens ENGAGED as the-being §3 promises. Contributor docs point at commands and extras that do not exist (README `python -m halbert_core.mcp`, INSTALLATION `main.py init`, `pip install -e halbert_core/` without the dashboard extra, `pytest tests/` on the wrong tree), and the root suite currently fails on main (2 files lack SPDX headers).

CONCEPT_MODEL: Halbert has three non-GUI faces, and each should present the same entity the dashboard does. Over MCP, Halbert is a peer agent that other agents consult about *this* machine: the initialize result should introduce it in the configured voice ("I am <ai_name>; these tools read my own state"), every finding should carry its four whys and provenance, knowledge search should reach the same indexed manuals the conversation uses, and write tools stay behind confirm + phrase. The MCP tool surface must read the same persisted stores the dashboard reads (or live inside the dashboard process), so what Halbert "tells you first" in the app is what it tells a coding agent. Body-to-body tool calls (PeerToolProxy) are the same MCP surface reached over the peer bearer token, which means one MCP mount reachable at the body's endpoint, not a second process nobody starts.

In the terminal, `halbert` is the entity's name and should open the conversation (or hand off to the desktop app); operational verbs (`halbert snapshot-configs`, `halbert dashboard`, `halbert license`) live under it; retired concepts (personas, legacy ChromaDB RAG) do not appear in help. For a contributor, one documented command installs the dashboard+dev extras, one documented command runs the real suite, and the knowledge index/corpus story (RAG-13/14) is a stated decision rather than a silent absence.

# F10 — p1-promise-vs-reality — do the founding docs and marketing site match what main (c36ae12e) delivers

SUMMARY: The biggest cohesion problem is that the two promises the marketing leads with — "It tells you first" and "I know 16,000 manuals, when I cite a page you can open it" — exist on main as primitives but do not reach a fresh install: the morning report is off unless the user finds a toggle in Settings › Identity & Voice, the four whys that findings carry (why_now/why_care/why_so/why_trust) are stored but no route lists them and no UI renders them, and the agent's document retrieval is gated on `import sourceprep`, which is not a dependency and is not importable in the project venv, so the chat path is assembled with retrieval=None. Second, "Cloud models and web search are switches, off by default" is contradicted by the code: `web_search` is registered unconditionally in the tool executor, classified SAFE, allowed by the default policy, handed to the model in every turn's tool schemas, and hits public DuckDuckGo/SearXNG; the feature flag that would turn it off has no consumer. Third, README.md contradicts the marketing thesis on the repo's front page: it calls Halbert "an AI assistant", says cloud hybrid is the default (the repo's own models.yml is all-Ollama), names specific vendor models against the founder's directive, and advertises spoken proactive alerts, fleet mesh, multi-session tabs and a PATH tracer that are 501, replaced, or unbuilt. What is genuinely wired and honest: no analytics or telemetry SDK exists, the dashboard binds 127.0.0.1, Tauri has no updater, the first-person identity prompt with the voice setting is in production, "how are you" triggers grounded reactive instructions plus a vitals module, the proactive SSE → bell chain is mounted in the merged shell, and the overnight Opus batch fixed the state-machine seams that would have broken these turns. "Every adjective has a number" is Linux-only and threshold-free: CPU temperature comes from psutil coretemp/k10temp (None on macOS), the hwmon ingester is a Phase-1 stub, the vitals module carries no thresholds, and no prompt text ties adjectives to measured limits. The provider story is close: Ollama, llama.cpp, MLX, Anthropic, OpenAI-compatible, LM Studio, Apple Intelligence and peer are handled by the production client, but the picker also offers Google and Azure (not chat-capable) and Apple Intelligence has no bridge source in the repo. FEATURES.md (dated December 2025) lists seven endpoints that do not exist and a sidebar UI that is gone. The top fixes are small and make the marketing honest without weakening it: a real web/cloud switch defaulting off, morning report on by default at onboarding, a findings list route plus why rendering in the bell and the Findings page, retrieval that works without a separately installed daemon (or a footnote saying it needs one), one corpus number, and a README rewrite in the marketing voice.

CONCEPT_MODEL: The product's public promise should read: Halbert is the machine speaking in first person from measured data. It notices before you ask — on Linux today, through detectors that watch the config tree and a daily report that is on by default — and every notice carries why it matters and where the evidence is. Every adjective it uses about itself is tied to a number it measured (temperature, load, disk, memory) with the threshold that makes the word true; where a platform cannot expose a sensor without privileges (macOS temperature), it says so instead of guessing. Nothing leaves the machine unless the operator flips a named switch: cloud model providers and web search are both off until configured, and there is no telemetry, analytics, or update check. Rationale lives next to change: when Halbert or the operator changes a config line, the reason is captured with the change and the evidence that motivated it, and it can be recalled by file, by line, and by date. Knowledge is grounded in a locally indexed corpus (one published number, currently ~29k documents) that ships or builds without a hidden dependency, and every citation opens the page it came from.

On platforms and models: Linux is the flagship; macOS is honest beta (discovery, identity, conversation and voice work; journald, config watching and sensor temperature are Linux-only until the Mac paths exist). Any OpenAI-compatible, Anthropic, Ollama, llama.cpp, MLX, LM Studio or paired-peer endpoint is a first-class choice; providers the code cannot call are not shown in the picker. Docs and README repeat the marketing voice and never name vendor models.

# F11 — w3-home-node-and-voice-appliance — the HOME journey on an N150-class node (install, wizard, compute peer, pairing, peer chat turn, HA event stream, /home page, proactive home events, voice appliance, Wyoming, HomeCognitiveLoop, Frigate)

SUMMARY: The biggest cohesion problem is that the home node has no agreed shape: HA strategy §4 Path 1 draws Halbert on the workstation and only HAOS on the edge node ("already built, zero new work"), while the singular-entity and home-simplification handoffs put a `home` Halbert body on the N150 (canonical memory host, voice, HA event stream), and main ships the second — but the documented install for it (deploy/halbert-home.service with `HALBERT_VARIANT=home` and a being.yml without `variant:`) gives the capability registry the SYSADMIN preset (reproduced: `_get_variant()`=home, registry enables discovery/ingestion/scheduler/terminal), so the N150 runs sysadmin services while its rail hides System. Second, nothing on the home body is proactive: HomeCognitiveLoop is never instantiated, the home preset turns the scheduler off, HA/Frigate events flush into cognition only when a user types a chat message, `proactive_speak` has no caller but a manual API route, `WYOMING_PROACTIVE_MIN_LEVEL` is read and never consumed, and the-being's proactive dial has no consumer on home — marketing's "It tells you first" is unmet on the kitchen body. Third, pairing is dead end to end in the UI even though the overnight API is correct and tested: mDNS returns a hardcoded `[]`, the Manual tab throws, no component lists or approves pending requests on the workstation, and the satellite drops the issued token into localStorage where the compute link never reads it — so a peer chat turn (which is genuinely wired and mounted now) works only after a curl-driven handshake plus a pasted token. Fourth, the family member in the kitchen exists in no anchor: the personas are Alex/Elena/Marcus, all five journeys are sysadmin or marketing, onboarding greets the home node with "your AI-powered Linux assistant", Settings shows all twelve tabs (Knowledge, System, Security, Debug) on the home body, Workloads (Apps/Network/Sharing) stays in the rail, and the Home page is a raw entity list with no areas, no why, and a light toggle that flips optimistically while the backend answers 403 at the default `observe` autonomy. The voice chain is wired on main and its tests pass, but `/voice` and `/voice-hud` are not in app.py's explicit SPA route list (404 from the systemd deployment), the display-power tiers are no-ops under a DISPLAY-less service on HDMI panels, audio is off until `audio_config.yml` and the `[audio-inference]` extras are installed (undocumented), Wyoming is loopback-only in practice because the HACS integration never sends the new `authenticate` frame, and no real audio has ever gone through. Frigate has eight mounted routes and a served `/frigate` SPA path with no React route, no rail item, an unbounded event queue, and snapshots that never reach the vision model. Two pairing directions with three token stores (models.yml endpoint api_key, being.yml peer_token that refuses to store without a canonical URL, localStorage) contradict the one-entity story in shell §9.5. The good news: the compute endpoint, the peer turn branch, the STT relay, the TTS egress hub, the pairing handshake, and the ComputePeerCard are all real on main, so the remaining work is mostly wiring, gating, and one persona decision rather than new subsystems.

CONCEPT_MODEL: Halbert on a home node is a body of the one Halbert, not a second product. The always-on node (N150) is the canonical host for memory and conversation and the voice/touch body; the workstation is the desk body and, when awake, the thinking body (compute). Both bodies pair once, mutually, with one credential, and the user only ever sees "Linked Devices" and body names — never nodes, peers, fleets, or tokens. On the home body the house is the physiology: HA entities and areas are its organs and rooms, HA history and Frigate events are its biography, the N150 is its nervous system; it still speaks in the first person as the computer, grounded in live HA state, and every proactive utterance carries its why (why now / why care / what I did / how to undo) and respects the proactive dial and quiet/guest hours. Capability, not variant, decides what a body can do (the variant is only the preset), and the UI — rail, Settings tabs, onboarding — is derived from the same capability set the backend launched, so the kitchen panel never shows a terminal, a RAG index, or a debug tab. A household member who is not the administrator is a first-class persona: voice-first, cannot approve, cannot configure, must be told honestly when the entity is degraded ("my desk body is asleep; I can't think right now") and must never see a UI that pretends an action happened.

# F12 — User journeys 2-5 (sysadmin: "how are you", proactive finding + approval, config archeology, marketing funnel) plus the terminal journey, walked end to end on main c36ae12e

SUMMARY: The biggest cohesion problem is that the two journeys the product is sold on (the-being.md §7 slices 1 and 2; marketing stops 01 and 04) are built as disconnected primitives that never join into an experience. Journey 3 (proactive finding to approval to execution) is 70% implemented in the backend, but the two joints that make it a journey are unwired: a detected finding is never turned into a proposal on the sweep path (generate_for_finding is called only from the MCP server and the unmounted somatic lifecycle), and the finding never reaches the conversation with its four whys (ProactiveEvent carries title/body only; the only surface is a bell dropdown inside the context stage, which is hidden in the founder-ruled default side-by-side layout). Even when a drop-in conflict is approved, execution is a verified no-op: the proposal is marked "applied" with an empty applied list and the finding stays open, because the drop-in change is a prose "requires manual review" item and WriteConfig only edits yaml/json/ini. Journey 4 ("why is SSH on 2222?") has neither a capture path nor a recall path in production: no write path records a reason, recall_memory is unimplemented and substituted by a manuals search, and the agent's context assembler deliberately excludes memory and the telemetry/self-knowledge/identity adapters that were built for exactly this. Journey 2 ("how are you") is the most cohesive: state-query trigger, vitals module summon, WhyChip provenance, and thread receipts all work, but grounding depends on the LLM choosing to call tools; there is no deterministic sensor read (the "Eyes" role), open findings are never in the prompt, and drive health is psutil partitions rather than SMART. The terminal journey's agent-run half is genuinely cohesive on main (tile in the turn, dock, timeline chip, R04 fixes verified merged), while the user-shell half ("watched by the AI", stage-into-shell, idle-terminal reuse) is scaffolding with no production caller, and a second legacy Terminal page forks the concept. Journey 5 in the methodology doc describes the archived pre-v7 site; v7's plates are static mockups of exactly the unwired journeys, and its early-access form never sends the email anywhere. Overnight fixes hold (turn cancel, reaper, pool leaks, chmod rollback confirmed on main); the gaps below are wiring and product joints, not regressions.

CONCEPT_MODEL: A Finding is a persisted, detector-produced fact about the host's configuration that carries its four whys (why now, why care, why so, why trust) and lives in findings.db; it is the unit the proactive channel delivers, the unit the conversation can talk about, and the unit a Proposal attaches to. A Proposal is a concrete, previewable change (diff or chmod) with dry-run, blast radius and a rollback handle; an Approval is the human decision on that proposal (dashboard, conversation, or MCP, all sharing one engine and one critical-tier phrase gate); Execution produces a Change Receipt (what changed, who approved, why, backup id) that is recorded in the same store recall reads from, so "why is this configured this way" is answered from the machine's own ledger rather than from a manuals search. The proactive dial and quiet hours gate delivery, not detection.

The conversation is the delivery surface for all of this (the-being.md: the conversation is the core layer, the dashboard runs under the hood). A proactive finding arrives as a turn in the conversation with its whys and three off-ramps (propose fix, snooze, dismiss), a severity dot on the Presence Pill when the context stage is collapsed, and the relevant module summoned alongside; "How are you" is answered after deterministic sensors run (the Eyes role: identity, load, disk, open findings) so every adjective has a number regardless of what the model chose to call. Terminals are one nervous system: the agent's commands appear as tiles in the turn, the admin's shells are watched (block boundaries recorded into the thread hint), and a proposed command is staged into the admin's prompt, never executed; there is one terminal concept, not a separate Terminal page.

# F13 — a1-module-map-and-boundaries — as-built package architecture of halbert_core vs the-being's three pillars (main @ c36ae12e)

SUMMARY: The biggest cohesion problem is that the presentation layer owns the governance pillar: dashboard/routes/settings.py (3,371 lines, 83 routes) holds the process singletons for ApprovalEngine, GuardrailEnforcer, DryRunSimulator, RecoveryExecutor and AnomalyDetector, and approvals are exposed through two HTTP APIs (/api/approvals and /api/settings/approvals/*) that the frontend straddles (lib/tauri.ts:133-151), while in-chat confirmations (/api/agent/confirm) bypass ApprovalEngine entirely, so "rationale persists" (the-being §2) is not true for decisions made in the conversation. Second, gating is three models at once: capabilities.py (used by app.py startup and the model layer), is_home_variant() still called from seven production sites, and an entirely dead config/feature_flags.py plus 39 ad-hoc HALBERT_* env gates; the CAP_SOURCEPREP probe tests `import sourceprep` although the backend talks to an HTTP daemon, which silently disables retrieval on this very machine (sourceprep is not importable in .venv). Third, of SourcePrep's three roles only retrieval is read in production; concepts and observations are write-only (findings/store.py:324,357) with no reader anywhere, so the "why" store the marketing promises ("I remember why you changed that") has no read path into the conversation. Fourth, federation/ is half product (pairing, peer auth, compute endpoint — wired, tested, mounted) and half Phase-9 scaffold (ComputeRouter, ComputeBroker, TelemetryAgent, mDNS, WoL, connectivity, FleetProxy — zero production instantiations), and the scaffold leaks into the UI: PeerPairingModal polls /api/peers/discovered every 10 s and it always returns [] (peers.py:598-603). Fifth, the Haloysius seam holds: haloysius is a declared extra, all 16 importing files import lazily, and the agent degrades to a plain chat agent without it; but the AppSeam is a consumer-side fiction (Haloysius never reads it), its ModelBackend is used only for LLM thoughts that are off by default, and the mind never learns about findings (no populate_cognition/add_worry anywhere in findings/, proactive/ or scheduler/), which breaks proof slice 1 ("the mind records it as conflicted_about"). Halbert also still carries three pre-Haloysius/pre-SourcePrep "self" layers on the chat path (knowledge/ 3.1k lines with 25 routes of which 3 are consumed; persona/ with its own IT_ADMIN/FRIEND enum next to being.yml; runtime/ placeholder graph of the three roles imported only by Halbert/main.py). The eager import graph is clean (one config<->obs 2-cycle, one {config, ingestion, obs} SCC caused by ingestion/redaction.py being a cross-cutting primitive under ingestion/), but five domain modules import upward into dashboard/ to reach singletons that live in route files. documentation/ARCHITECTURE.md (last touched 2026-08-30) describes none of this: zero mentions of Haloysius or the state machine, cites a routes/chat.py that does not exist, and calls ChromaDB the memory system when memory/__init__.py itself says HybridMemorySystem is eval-only. All recommended fixes below are fences, deletions of dead code, or singleton moves that keep every URL stable — none requires a rewrite and all fit the "finish current features first" direction.

CONCEPT_MODEL: Halbert is one FastAPI process per body (halbert_core/dashboard/app.py) in which three kinds of code live. The Guide is agents/state_machine.py (3,545 lines) driving one conversation over one SQLite thread store (agents/conversation_sqlite.py, agents/threads.py) with Halbert-owned memory-across-time in continuity/ (founder D1). Its mind is the external Haloysius package, reached only through integrations/{app_seam, cognition_wiring, modality_wiring, state_trackers, haloysius_memory_adapter}: an optional extra whose absence degrades Halbert to a chat agent with no worries, drives, state trackers or voice modality. The Eyes and Hands are discovery/ (42 scanner files), ingestion/ (journald, hwmon, redaction), config/ (registry, watcher, drift, snapshot, sensitivity), findings/detectors, alerts/, vision/, audio/ and system/; the Deep Thinker is scheduler/autonomous_tasks (DetectorSweep, MorningReport, HealthCheck) plus proactive/ and agents/subagents. They report into the one mind through integrations/system_event_mapper.py — today only discovery/service events do; findings do not. Awareness and rationale are SourcePrep, behind context/adapters.SourcePrepAdapter → integrations/sourceprep_retrieval_backend (read) and integrations/sourceprep_client (concepts/observations, currently write-only). The governance stack that must sit between mind and action is tools/safety + tools/executor, approval/, autonomy/, policy/, findings/proposals — the code the-being says Halbert "keeps and deepens" — but its singletons and half its HTTP surface currently live inside dashboard/routes/settings.py.

Bodies of one entity are linked by federation/ (peers_config, peer_middleware, compute_endpoint, tool_allowlist are product; compute_router, compute_broker, telemetry_agent, peer_discovery, wake_on_lan, connectivity, fleet_proxy are unwired scaffold) and by agents/peer_tool_proxy (built into ToolExecutor, never injected). capabilities.py decides what a body runs (10 capabilities, 6 probed) and is meant to be the only gate; variant survives only as a preset and a UI role label. dashboard/routes/ (36 modules, 322 routes; 181 with a UI consumer) should be a thin HTTP skin over these packages, with mcp/server.py as the second, redacted skin for external clients and peers. That is the architecture as it should read now; the items below are the places where the code still diverges from it.
